# Bot Mode Parity

> [!WARNING]
> This row ledger audits an older Hermes Bot Mode implementation. Current work
> must use the exact upstream pin and completion rules in
> [`PARITY-BASELINE.md`](PARITY-BASELINE.md). Do not quote the percentages below
> as current coverage until this ledger is regenerated.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **Current canonical-session correction (Hermes `a4f16e3f`).** Every row below
> that describes `ui_meta["hermes-bots"].chat`, `preferred_session_ids`,
> `preferred_session`, grandfathering, or a client-maintained canonical pin is
> retained only as superseded historical audit evidence. Current Hermes owns a
> unique `(profile, root_title: "Bot Chat")` registry and returns
> `canonical_session`; bot-row opens independently exact-list
> `{profile,title:"Bot Chat",include_hidden:true}`. Talaria never uses the
> legacy metadata pointer, recency, or the last visible session as canonical
> identity. The maintained contract and tests are documented in
> [`CURRENT-HERMES-CANONICAL-SESSION.md`](CURRENT-HERMES-CANONICAL-SESSION.md).

**Talaria's primary reference is Hermes Desktop's Bot Mode**, the in-tree plugin at
`hermes-agent-upstream/apps/desktop/src/plugins/hermes-bots/plugin.js` (8,323 lines).

> "Full parity with the bot plugin, with full emphasis there to create a beautiful user
> experience — which is why we didn't create a session-style UI/UX. Bot-plugin first, but
> with all of the core hermes desktop functionality behind it."
> — maintainer, on what Talaria is

This document is the implementable spec for that parity.

1. [Why this document](#1-why-this-document)
2. [The model](#2-the-model) — read this first
3. [The regions](#3-the-regions) — all 443 audited rows
4. [Design notes](#4-design-notes) — the craft details; matching the *feel* is the point
5. [Build order](#5-build-order) — phases 1/2/3 with first steps
6. [Mobile ceiling](#6-mobile-ceiling) — what iOS cannot do, and what it merely hasn't done yet

---

## 1. Why this document

Talaria is not a session manager with bots bolted on. It is Bot Mode, on a phone, with the
rest of Hermes Desktop standing behind it. That single decision explains the whole shape of
the app: a roster instead of a sidebar, a forever-chat per bot instead of a session list, a
tab bar instead of a tiling workspace. Every time Talaria drifts toward "list of sessions"
it stops being the product.

Keeping that honest requires knowing exactly what the reference does — not from memory, not
from a README, but from the source. So a **seven-region deep audit** was run over the plugin
on **17–18 August 2026**, reading `plugin.js` line by line and checking each behavior against
`/Users/joshua/talaria/app/Packages/Talaria/Sources/`. It produced **443 rows**, each citing a
`plugin.js:LINE`, plus 130 `designNotes` capturing the craft details — cadences, copy strings,
ordering rules, the reasons behind the defaults. All of it is reproduced below. Nothing was
summarized away.

Two things landed in Talaria on the audit day and are verified present in the tree:

- **The naming rules.** `Bot.displayTitle` / `.handle` / `.showsHandle`
  (`TalariaKit/Models.swift:145,179,207`) are verbatim ports of `displayName()` (plugin.js:2935)
  and `botHandle()` (plugin.js:2406), including the `default` → **Hermes** / `@hermes` rule.
- **The cosmetics bridge, read half.** `TalariaKit/BotModeMeta.swift` parses the server-side
  `ui_meta["hermes-bots"]` block (title, shape, color, groups) and maps desktop's shape and
  color vocabulary onto Talaria's, so a bot renamed or recolored on the laptop reads the same
  on the phone.

**Pets** (`TalariaKit/PetModels.swift`, `TalariaUI/GatewayClient+Pets.swift`) are being
implemented in parallel; the four pet rows in Region 2 are marked *in flight* rather than
missing.

One rule decided every status: **no gateway was reachable during the run**, so a row is ✅ only when the behaviour is device-local and provable by reading the code and building it, and everything that depends on a gateway's actual response shape is 🔶 carrying the words *"built but never run against a live gateway"* — however complete the implementation looks. Those notes are the first-session checklist for a real gateway, not hedging.

---

## 2. The model

Read this before touching any of the tables. Bot Mode is five ideas; everything else in the
plugin is those five ideas being defended.

### A bot *is* a profile

There is no bot object. `profiles.list` returns Hermes profiles, and Bot Mode paints each one
as a roster row (plugin.js:2218). The profile directory *is* the roster; creating a bot creates
a profile, deleting a bot deletes the profile and its directory. Everything a bot "has" — its
SOUL.md, its model, its skills, its MCP servers, its cron jobs — is profile configuration
reached through the same RPCs the rest of Hermes uses. What Bot Mode adds is a *presentation*:
a name you'd say out loud, a face, a single conversation, and an inbox.

The primary profile is literally named `default`, which "reads like nobody bothered", so it is
special-cased in three places — it renders as **Hermes**, it is tagged **@hermes**, and it gets a
fixed friendly violet squircle instead of a hash-derived near-black square (plugin.js:2935,
2406, and the avatar path). Any surface that shows a profile name needs all three.

### The canonical forever-chat — and why there is no session UI

**Each bot has exactly one chat, forever.** Current Hermes identifies it by a
server-owned unique title registry row: `(profile, root_title: "Bot Chat")`.
`profiles.list {include_sessions:true}` returns that row as `canonical_session`,
including its durable root id and resolved compression tip. Opening a bot does
not trust a cached pointer: every bot-row tap independently calls
`session.list {profile,title:"Bot Chat",include_hidden:true}`, exact-filters the
root/legacy leaf title, resumes the resolved tip, and retains the durable root
for identity and `/new` protection. A failed or indeterminate lookup propagates;
it never authorizes creation.

Birth is single-flighted per source-qualified profile. It rechecks the exact
registry, creates a hidden session, applies the exact title before local
adoption, and only then submits `"Hey, tell me about yourself!"`. A definite
kickoff failure preserves the named row for the next open; ambiguous acceptance
retains exact route/session ownership for read-back reconciliation. Ordinary
`last_session` recency, CLI/cron activity, and legacy `ui_meta.chat` never become
adoption evidence. Older gateways may omit `canonical_session`; omission and
malformed current data are inconclusive and preserve the last authoritative
guard identity, while explicit `null` alone clears it.

Two corollaries fall out of this:

- **Bot Mode sessions are always hidden** (`hidden:true` on create, plus a `session.set_hidden`
  reconciliation sweep on every load and reconnect). Hidden means *owned*, not secret: a
  six-member group room would otherwise dump six identical rows into recents. They stay fully
  browsable in the one surface that owns them, via `session.list {include_hidden:true}`.
- **`/new` and `/reset` typed inside the canonical chat become `/compact`**, with the toast
  *"This chat never resets — Bot chats are one continuous conversation — compacting instead. For a
  throwaway session with this agent, use Sessions mode."* (plugin.js:8219-8242). The guard is
  scoped to the canonical chat only; scratch sessions on the same profile keep full `/new` freedom.

This is why Talaria has no session-style UI. A session list is the wrong primary noun; the bot
is. The session browser exists — desktop's replaces the pane content with a Back button rather
than opening a modal or changing routes — but **the primary tap must never land on a list of
sessions.**

### Two identities: a title and an @handle

Every row prints a bold **display name** and, *only when they differ*, a quiet monospaced
**@handle** at 0.6875rem in the quaternary text color (`showsHandle`). The handle is never the
loud thing — it exists so you know what to type. `displayName()` prefers a user-set title, maps
`default` → Hermes, and otherwise de-slugs and title-cases the profile name; `botHandle()` is the
profile name, except `default` → `hermes` (plugin.js:2935, 2406). Both are ported verbatim into
`Bot.displayTitle` / `Bot.handle`.

The handle is the addressing primitive: `@handle` in any composer resolves against the live
roster and turns into a real delivery. Ambiguity **refuses rather than guesses** — when two bots
share a bare name that form resolves to nothing and the user must type `@name-device`, because
silently picking one would send a real message to the wrong machine (plugin.js:2434-2470).

### Cosmetics live on the server, with a local overlay

Per-bot look and metadata live in one `$botMeta` map keyed by profile name —
`{shape, color, image, imageKind, title, custom, pinned, group, chat, created}` — written through
`saveBotMeta` (plugin.js:201-270), which fans out to **three** stores:

1. **plugin-local** `ctx.storage['bot-meta']` — instant, offline, per-machine;
2. **server** `profiles.configure {ui_meta:{'hermes-bots': …}}` — with `image`/`pet` stripped,
   because `ui_meta` has a 64 KB cap and rides *every* `profiles.list`;
3. **the profile asset store** `profiles.set_asset {asset:'avatar'}` — uncapped, for actual image
   bytes.

`mergeServerMeta` overlays server `ui_meta` on every roster poll while preserving the local-only
image, and treats an *omitted* `chat` key as an authoritative deletion (plugin.js:432-482). The
write returns a **three-valued outcome** — `persisted` / `unsupported` / `failed` — so an old
gateway that simply doesn't speak the contract produces no toast at all, while a gateway that
speaks it and says no does. Only a real remote failure is worth the user's attention.

Talaria reads this block correctly today and writes its own `ui_meta["talaria"]`. **The bridge is
one-way**, which means a recolor on the phone is invisible on the laptop and desktop's next save
wins silently. Making it symmetric is a Phase 1 item.

### Multi-source rosters

The roster is a union, not a list. `useRoster` merges the active gateway's rich `profiles.list`
with `host.agents()` across every registered Connection (plugin.js:2218-2394): same-source rows
are annotated in place, other-source rows are appended as thin `remoteSource` rows carrying a
device label. Duplicate names get precomputed `name-device` handles, and identity is a
source-qualified `botRosterKey` (`connectionId::name`).

Foreign rows are **visibly second-class, and that is intentional**: a bare connection-label chip,
no context menu, no unread, no local metadata (two "default" agents must never borrow each
other's title, color or pin), and tapping does not navigate — it explains: *"Stay in this chat and
@handle to message them. Gateway stays on this device."*

Talaria binds one gateway at a time through `ConnectionRegistry`, so the union roster does not
exist yet. **21 of the 52 ➖ rows are n/a for that reason alone** — they are unbuilt, not
impossible (see §6).

### The signal model, in one paragraph

Presence and unread are **poll-derived, not event-derived**. Every 5 s, `profiles.list` returns
each profile's `last_session.last_active`; `trackInboundActivity` (plugin.js:114) diffs it against
a per-bot watermark seeded on the first poll. Any timestamp that moves past the watermark for a
bot that isn't the one on screen raises an unread dot. That is why the badge catches *every*
delivery path — RPC, CLI, a bot-to-bot handoff, a cron run, another machine, work done while the
phone was asleep — and why an event-only model is structurally blind to all of them. Toasts are a
deliberately opt-in second layer, default **off**, because a busy roster is a firehose and the dot
already carries the signal.

## 3. The regions

443 rows, every one carrying its `plugin.js` citation. Rows sort ⭕ missing → 🔶 partial → ✅ present
→ ➖ n/a, and inside each status by value then effort, so the top of every table is the work that
matters most for the least effort. Each region opens with the auditor's own synthesis of what the
plugin does and where Talaria stands.

### Region 1 — Roster rows

**73 rows** — ⭕ 26 missing · 🔶 34 partial · ✅ 8 present · ➖ 5 n/a on mobile

Exhaustive parity audit of Hermes Bot Mode's roster surface — row anatomy, the live "math face" avatar engine, activity/unread semantics, pinning, multi-source connection badges, ordering, and every empty/error/stale state — read from plugin.js lines 75–1390 (avatars, meta persistence, activity watermarks), 2200–2260 + 2400–2430 + 2660–2730 + 2935–3007 (roster query, identity, preview text), 3775–4112 (BotRow), 6888–6937 (Active-now strip), 7489–7610 (GroupRow) and 7612–7979 (BotsPane ordering/states), checked against Talaria's RosterView/AvatarView/AppModelLive. Headline: the naming rules (displayName/botHandle/showsHandle) and the READ half of the ui_meta["hermes-bots"] bridge are genuinely ported and correct; Talaria's row anatomy, entrance animation and theming are strong and in places richer than desktop. The gaps are structural, not cosmetic: (1) no recency ordering at all — Talaria paints profiles.list in the gateway's directory order, where desktop's whole roster is messaging-app ranked by max(created, last_active) with pinned bots floated; (2) no pinning; (3) no 90-second liveness window — "working" is purely event-driven, so a bot that just spoke from cron/CLI/another machine looks idle; (4) no roster-poll unread watermark, so unread only counts messages that arrive while the app is attached and foregrounded; (5) no bot-to-bot ("Message from 🤖 …") preview treatment, which is the single most distinctive thing about a Bot Mode row; (6) no per-row context menu (pin/sessions/edit/group/duplicate/delete) — desktop's entire row-level command surface is absent; (7) the canonical-chat pin (ui_meta chat) is parsed into BotModeMeta.pinnedChat but never used, so tapping a bot opens "most recent session", the exact drift desktop wrote a 40-line comment to prevent; (8) the cosmetics bridge is read-only — Talaria writes ui_meta["talaria"], so a phone recolor is invisible on desktop; (9) no multi-source union roster, so no connection-label chips. The avatar engine is a deliberate simplification (6 static silhouettes vs 7 legacy shapes + 5 platonic solids + procedural sigils + a 15fps morphing face with gaze, tilt, catchlights and thinking dots) — recommended as a partial-parity target, not a full port, but the dark-body eye/catchlight flip and the working eye-widen are cheap wins.

Descending. A freshly created bot therefore tops the roster until another bot receives a message. Rescans the document (including shadow roots, plugin.js:1095-1107) at most 1Hz to find svg[data-hb-math]. An IntersectionObserver tracks visibility; only visible faces are painted. The loop PARKS when zero faces are mounted or zero visible, and is woken by a face mounting (BotFace calls startFaceClock on render) or one scrolling in. stopFaceClock on plugin dispose cancels the frame, disconnects the observer and drops all cached nodes — before that hook existed the loop ran until app restart. | 🔶 partial<br>`TalariaTheme/AvatarView.swift:108-119` | M · high | Talaria runs a detached Task per AvatarView for blinking plus per-view SwiftUI repeatForever animations (AvatarView.swift:108-119, 132-139). | **Server-avatar pull/push reconciliation** | `plugin.js:295-341, 394-430`<br>_profiles.get_asset / profiles.set_asset / has_avatar_ | Per poll: (push) any row WITHOUT has_avatar whose local meta holds a data: image uploads it via profiles.set_asset then invalidates the roster; a row with no image anywhere gets its live SVG rasterized at 160 and pushed, so notices have a pfp. (pull) any row WITH has_avatar and no local image fetches profiles.get_asset once and caches it into bot-meta — unless it sniffs as a 160px backfill and the bot is not a photo/pet. No push/backfill half (Talaria has no local-only images to reconcile), and has_avatar from profiles.list is parsed (GatewayClient.swift:50) but never consulted, so the first render of every bot still costs a get_asset round trip that could be skipped. |

Hydration is defensive — Promise.resolve around a storage getter that may be sync, async or absent, inside a try, and live in-memory values win over stored ones on merge so a hydrate cannot clobber a save that landed first. | 🔶 partial<br>`TalariaUI/AppModelLive.swift:166-192` | S · medium | Talaria has no local bot-meta store at all — everything is server-derived, which is arguably cleaner on a phone. Drop and cloud are sampled from their original bezier/arc paths (sampleDropRing 823, sampleCloudRing 892 with a hand-rolled SVG-arc-to-center converter at 843) so they keep their exact silhouettes. SwiftUI's correct analogue is a static Shape plus a transform modifier — Talaria's AvatarSilhouette (percentage-based polygons, AvatarView.swift:8-41) is the right architecture. Port the shape ROSTER, not this machinery. |

### Region 2 — Bot CRUD, cosmetics and the meta store

**73 rows** — ⭕ 24 missing · 🔶 28 partial · ✅ 13 present · ➖ 8 n/a on mobile

Exhaustive read of the bot CRUD + cosmetics region of hermes-bots/plugin.js (lines 197-605, 606-1390, 1641-2216, 2400-2990, 3748-3840, 3843-4112, 4936-5962, 7620-7979, 7983-8160) against Talaria's Sources/. The desktop model is: one `$botMeta` map keyed by profile name holding {shape, color, image, imageKind, title, custom, pinned, group, chat, created}, written through `saveBotMeta` which fans out to THREE stores — plugin-local ctx.storage ('bot-meta'), server `profiles.configure {ui_meta:{'hermes-bots':…}}` (image/pet stripped, 64KB cap), and the uncapped profile asset store `profiles.set_asset {asset:'avatar'}` for the actual image — and returns a three-valued outcome (persisted / unsupported / failed) so only a REAL remote failure toasts. `mergeServerMeta` overlays server ui_meta on every roster poll while preserving the local-only image and treating an omitted `chat` as authoritative deletion. Around that sit: an avatar picker with four tabs (Bot shapes+colors, Generate via image.generate, Upload from device, Pet from the petdex gallery), a create dialog that lazily materializes a DRAFT profile (discarded on cancel, re-created on rename) so MCP OAuth/API-key setup works during creation, a duplicate that clones the profile and the look with a "(copy)" title into the first free `<base>-N` slot, and a delete that prefers the SDK's teardown-routed deleteProfile over the racy CLI path and then scrubs six pieces of plugin-local state. Talaria has the honest half of this: a genuinely better editor contract (profiles.describe → dirty diff, so a save can never blank SOUL.md), portrait generate/clear, duplicate, and the just-landed displayName/botHandle + ui_meta["hermes-bots"] READ bridge. What is missing is the write side of that bridge (Talaria writes ui_meta["talaria"], so a phone recolor is invisible on desktop), title editing, delete, pin, image upload, pets, the shape/color vocabulary (6 vs 8 shapes, 6 vs 10 colors), the primary-profile look override, the persisted/local/failed distinction, and the draft-profile create flow.

| Feature | Plugin source | Behavior | Talaria | Effort | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Edit Profile — Title field** | `plugin.js:5076-5083, 4995`<br>_ui_meta['hermes-bots'].title_ | Labeled 'Title', an Input whose PLACEHOLDER is `displayName(bot, null)` — i.e. the auto-derived name, so leaving it blank visibly means 'keep the derived name'. Saved trimmed into meta.title (empty string clears it). | ⭕ missing<br>`TalariaKit/Models.swift:52-55` | S · critical | Bot.title is READ from the desktop block (AppModelLive.swift:188) and used by displayTitle, but nothing in Talaria can set it. A bot titled on desktop reads right on the phone; a bot the user wants to title from the phone cannot be. Highest-value single missing field in this region. |
| **Roster ordering — recency where 'activity' includes creation** | `plugin.js:7637-7666`<br>_profiles.list + ui_meta.created_ | activityOf(bot) = max(meta.created \|\| ui_meta['hermes-bots'].created \|\| 0, last_session.last_active*1000). Messaging-app order: most recent first. A freshly created bot therefore TOPS the list until another bot gets a message. Explicitly no special slot for the primary bot — it competes on recency too. Pinned-ness is the only override. | ⭕ missing<br>`TalariaUI/Screens/RosterView.swift:93`<br>_in flight (PetModels.swift)_ | S · critical | Talaria renders model.bots in whatever order profiles.list returned. Sorting by max(created, lastActive) with pinned first is the highest-leverage single change to make the roster feel alive. |
| **Upload from device** | `plugin.js:1684-1708, 1954-1962`<br>_client-only → profiles.set_asset_ | Tab content is a single full-width secondary Button with a 'device-camera' Codicon and copy 'Choose an image…'. It builds a hidden <input type=file accept='image/png,image/jpeg,image/webp,image/gif'>, rejects >15,000,000 bytes with the toast 'Image too large (max 15MB).', and reads the file as a data URL. | ⭕ missing<br>`TalariaUI/Components/AttachmentTray.swift:340-373` | M · critical | No avatar upload path. The iOS analogue is already proven in-repo: AttachmentTray's PhotosPicker (matching:.images, photoLibrary:.shared()). | **deleteBot — preferred path and why** | `plugin.js:526-549`<br>_cli.exec (no profiles.delete RPC exists)_ | Prefer `host.deleteProfile(name)` when the shell ships it: it routes through the Electron-intercepted REST delete which tears down the bot's pool backend FIRST and routes the next request away from it. The older `cli.exec ['profile','delete',name,'--yes']` path bypasses that interception — a backend that the roster's hover pre-warm just woke (right-click hovers the row!) holds the profile dir open, the CLI's rmtree races the live backend, and the renderer's socket reconnect respawns it mid-delete, resurrecting the directory. That is the famous 'can't delete a bot' error. CLI failure is detected via `result.blocked \|\| result.code !== 0` and thrown as result.hint \|\| result.output \|\| `Could not delete profile ${name}.` | ⭕ missing<br>`TalariaTheme/CopyPack.swift:157` | M · critical | Talaria refuses deletion outright: deleteNote = 'Deleting a profile is CLI-only: hermes profile delete (name).' That was honest before, but cli.exec IS available over the gateway (ws-protocol.md:671, {argv,timeout≤600} → {blocked,hint,code,output}) — so the phone CAN delete. It must adopt the teardown ordering caveat: Talaria pre-warms nothing on long-press, so the race is milder, but a delete should still be followed by a roster refetch and a check that the row is gone. |

Thin rows from another source (remoteSource) get NO context menu at all — their profile metadata is not loaded, so edit/delete/pin would mutate whichever backend happens to be active; a normal click activates the owner and the refreshed rich row then exposes the menu. | ⭕ missing<br>`TalariaUI/Screens/RosterView.swift:93-99` | M · critical | Talaria roster rows have tap-to-open only — no long-press menu anywhere (only SessionsSheet.swift:308-317 uses swipeActions/contextMenu). Returns `{serverPersisted, serverOutcome}`. The whole point (comment at 248) is that 'unsupported' is the documented legacy fallback and must NEVER toast, or every save on a legacy setup errors forever. | ⭕ missing<br>`TalariaUI/GatewayClient+Profiles.swift:215-220` | S · high | applyProfileEdit already returns the per-section `applied` map but every caller discards it (@discardableResult; saveProfileEdit only checks for a thrown error). ### Region 3 — The canonical chat

**58 rows** — ⭕ 17 missing · 🔶 29 partial · ✅ 7 present · ➖ 5 n/a on mobile

The plugin's central product decision is that **every bot has exactly ONE forever-chat**, addressed by a durable stored-session id pinned in the profile's server-side `ui_meta["hermes-bots"].chat` (plugin.js:2726-2738). Opening a bot NEVER resumes "the most recent session" — that would drift the moment the profile is used from the CLI, Sessions mode, or a cronjob. Instead `openBotCanonicalChat` (plugin.js:2822) runs a four-branch resolver: (1) no pin + existing history → *grandfather-adopt* the session the row already previews and pin it; (2) live pin → verify through the gateway's precise `preferred_session` resolver (`profiles.list {preferred_session_ids}`), open `resolved_id` (the compression-lineage tip) but keep the durable pin; (3) lookup failure (older gateway / transient) → the pin is innocent until proven guilty, try it as-is, only a *rejected open* clears it; (4) `preferred_session === null` (definitively gone) → re-anchor on the previewed session, else clear and create. Creation (plugin.js:2748) mints `session.create {profile, title:'Bot Chat', hidden:true}`, pins `stored_session_id` immediately, mounts the view, waits 400 ms, then fires the kickoff `"Hey, tell me about yourself!"` so the chat is born with the bot introducing itself (empty sessions get pruned server-side, so pre-creating at enable time is impossible). Bot Mode sessions are ALWAYS hidden from the global sidebar (`hidden:true` on create + a `session.set_hidden` reconciliation sweep on every load/reconnect); the per-profile Sessions workspace is the only place they surface, via `session.list {include_hidden:true}`. `/new` and `/reset` typed inside the canonical chat are rewritten to `/compact` with the toast "This chat never resets".

> **Updated 2026-08-18 — the canonical-chat model is now ported.** Roadmap Phase 0 landed `TalariaUI/AppModelLive+CanonicalChat.swift`, a 482-line resolver that is the single door every open and every send funnels through, in the plugin's own order: explicit binding → **pin** → canonical title → previewed session → birth (`session.create {title: 'Bot Chat', hidden: true}`, pinned immediately). The pin is read from and **written back to** `ui_meta["hermes-bots"].chat`, so phone and desktop open the same conversation. Every entry point in the app now routes through `openChat(botID:)`. Two divergences are deliberate and documented at their sites: the pin is verified by resuming it rather than through `profiles.list {preferred_session_ids}` (one round trip, and the answer describes the exact operation), and the kickoff intro is not fired (a tap on a phone should not spend a model turn nobody asked for). **None of it has been run against a live gateway**, so all fourteen moved rows are 🔶, not ✅. The paragraph below is the pre-run finding, kept because it is the description of the bug the phase existed to fix.
>
> Two rows here also record real remaining gaps: there is still no `set_hidden` reconciliation sweep, and `/new` is still offered inside the canonical chat with no `/compact` reroute.

**The pre-run finding (2026-08-17): the canonical-chat model is essentially not ported.** `BotModeMeta` parses `ui_meta["hermes-bots"].chat` into `pinnedChat` (TalariaKit/BotModeMeta.swift:20,34) but **no code reads it** — a project-wide grep finds zero consumers. `refreshRoster` instead caches `profile.last_session.id` as `lastSessionByBot` (AppModelLive.swift:167-169) and `ensureSession` resumes exactly that (AppModelLive.swift:250-273) — i.e. Talaria implements the precise "most recent session" behavior the plugin exists to avoid. This compounds badly: the gateway's `last_session` is built by `list_sessions_rich(...)` with `include_hidden` defaulting to **False** (tui_gateway/methods_profiles.py:131-173, hermes_state.py:8665), so a bot whose only conversation is its (hidden) canonical Bot Chat reports `last_session: null` to Talaria. On any gateway that desktop Bot Mode has touched, Talaria therefore (a) shows no preview, (b) cannot see the Bot Chat in its session list (`listSessions` sends no `include_hidden`, GatewayClient.swift:279-284), and (c) **creates a brand-new untitled session on first send, silently forking the user's forever-chat**. `createSession` also sends no `title` and no `hidden` (GatewayClient.swift:286-293), so each Talaria-born chat lands visible and untitled in the desktop sidebar. There is no kickoff intro, no single-flight guard, no `preferred_session_ids` verification, no `set_hidden` sweep, and `/new` is offered as a plain slash command with no canonical-chat guard (AppModelLive+Commands.swift:373). The one genuinely-ported piece is the cosmetics bridge (title/shape/color) at AppModelLive.swift:173-188, which is correct and reads desktop meta ahead of Talaria's own. Talaria's SessionsSheet is a strong analogue of the Sessions workspace (it adds cross-session FTS, rename, delete — beyond desktop) but it is missing `include_hidden`, so it cannot browse the very sessions Bot Mode owns.

| Feature | Plugin source | Behavior | Talaria | Effort | Notes |

### Region 4 — Handles, search and mentions

**54 rows** — ⭕ 23 missing · 🔶 17 partial · ✅ 3 present · ➖ 11 n/a on mobile

Bot Mode treats every roster row as a two-part identity — a customizable **display name** and a stable **@handle** — and builds four surfaces on top of that: (1) a multi-source roster that unions the active gateway's rich `profiles.list` with `host.agents()` across every registered Connection, annotating same-source rows in place and appending other-source rows as thin `remoteSource` rows tagged with their device label; (2) duplicate-name disambiguation via precomputed `name-device` handles plus a source-qualified `botRosterKey` used as the React key and cross-surface identity; (3) `@handle` resolution in prose — a composer middleware that parses mentions outside code fences, resolves them against the roster (ambiguous forms deliberately resolve to nothing, forcing the `@name-device` form), then either appends a CLI handoff instruction block for local bots or fires a background gateway-routed DM for Connections bots; and (4) an `@`-autocomplete provider registered into every composer, prefix-matching handles from the ≤5s-stale roster cache, capped at 8. `filterBots` search matches display name, profile name, handle and device label, strips a leading `@`, and never re-ranks.

Talaria has landed the identity core faithfully — `Bot.displayTitle` / `Bot.handle` / `Bot.showsHandle` are verbatim ports (Models.swift:145/179/207), the `ui_meta["hermes-bots"]` title/shape/color bridge reads desktop's block first (BotModeMeta.swift + AppModelLive.swift:173), and the A2A attribution prefix regexes are ported (AppModelLive+Feeds.swift:953/969). Everything downstream of identity was missing when this region was audited. **Phase C (2026-08-18) closed the mention half:** a bot chat now runs desktop's composer middleware on every send — the fast gate, the code-stripping resolver, the sticky ambiguity refusal — and delivers the handoff over the gateway instead of submitting `@ops` to the model as literal characters (`routeMentions`, AppModelLive+A2A.swift:470; the grammar, the form map and the note live in TalariaKit/BotMention.swift and are pinned by `talaria-verify`). **Phase C also mounted the completion surface in the chat composer** — the same synchronous prefix-on-handle provider desktop registers into every composer, now with desktop's `Bot · <display name>` meta line (`[Bot].mentionSuggestions`, TalariaKit/BotMention.swift:334; the strip is Components/MentionField.swift:122, mounted at ChatView.swift:821 and by the handoff sheet). **Phase C also landed the `@name-device` rule end to end:** the form is minted for BOTH sides of a collision (`AgentHandle.mint`, a port of connection-registry.ts:137-141, applied to foreign rows by `ConnectionRegistry.foreignRoster` and to the live gateway's own rows by `AppModel.liveRosterBots`), offered by the completion provider with the device on its meta line, and parsed back by a resolver that reads the union roster — so a handle this app mints is a handle this app resolves, pinned as a round trip by `talaria-verify`. Still open in this region: Talaria holds ONE live socket, so a foreign row is addressable but not deliverable — the mention path names it (`MentionResolution.unreachable`) instead of carrying to it, where desktop routes over Connections (plugin.js:2593). The row-by-row status below is authoritative. Two smaller regressions: BotSheetView.swift:57 rolls its own display-name rule that ignores `title`, and there is no title editor anywhere in the app, so the desktop-set title is read-only on the phone.

| Feature | Plugin source | Behavior | Talaria | Effort | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **resolveRosterMentions() — prose extraction and token rules** | `plugin.js:2436, 2470`<br>_client-only_ | Prose = text with ```fenced blocks``` and `inline code` replaced by a space FIRST, so a handle inside code never triggers a handoff. Token regex `/(^\|\s)@([a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*)/gi` — an @ must be at string start or preceded by whitespace (so 'user@host' and 'a@b' are not mentions), the first char must be alphanumeric, and the body allows [a-z0-9_-] (NO dots — unlike the group-chat parser). Token lowercased. The 'hermes' token is passed through unchanged unless byForm has it (effectively a no-op guard, since botHandle already maps a non-active 'default' row to 'hermes'). Unresolvable tokens are silently skipped. Results deduped by botRosterKey and returned in first-mention order. | ✅ present<br>`TalariaKit/BotMention.swift:27` | M · critical | Landed 2026-08-18 (Phase C). `BotMention.prose` / `.tokens(in:)` are the port, in TalariaKit so the composer, the handoff sheet and the verify suite share one grammar: fenced blocks then inline code each replaced by a single SPACE (the space is load-bearing — it supplies the boundary the token regex needs), then `(^|\s)@([a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*)` case-insensitive, capture lowercased, duplicates kept for the resolver to dedupe by bot. Pinned by `ProtocolChecks+Mentions.swift` — `user@example.com` and `a@b` are not mentions, a dot ends a handle, an unterminated backtick cannot swallow the message, and a fenced handle stays literal text end to end. |
| **resolveRosterMentions() — form map construction** | `plugin.js:2434`<br>_client-only_ | Builds `byForm: Map<string, bot\|null>`. For each roster member: skip rows with no name and skip the bot already speaking (isActiveRosterBot). Collect forms = Set([botHandle(name,bot).toLowerCase(), name.toLowerCase()]) plus bot.handle.toLowerCase() when present. For each form: if the map already holds a DIFFERENT bot for that form, set it to null (permanently ambiguous — the bare name of a duplicated profile stops resolving, forcing the user to type the @name-device form); if unset, set it. Note the ambiguity poison is sticky: once null, a third bot with the same form cannot claim it. | ✅ present<br>`TalariaKit/BotMention.swift:184` | L · critical | Landed 2026-08-18 (Phase C). `MentionResolver.resolve` builds the same map from the same forms (handle, raw name, and `handleOverride` when present), skipping nameless rows and the speaker, and the ambiguity poison is STICKY exactly as upstream: a form two bots claimed is dropped and a third cannot take it, so a duplicated bare name resolves to NOBODY and the user must type the @name-device form. One addition: the phone also remembers WHICH bots collided (`MentionCollision`), because it has to say so — see the middleware row. Pinned by `ProtocolChecks+Mentions.swift`, including the sticky third-claimant case and the @name-device escape hatch. |

It runs BEFORE the attachment rewrite deliberately: those refs are `@file:<path>`, which a later scan would read as a handle. **The cold-cache fallback (8258-8283) is deliberately not ported.** Upstream's `useRoster` caches under `[...ROSTER_KEY, activeConnectionId]` (2222) while the middleware reads the un-suffixed `ROSTER_KEY` (8253), so in practice desktop takes the fallback on every send and treats every mention as local; Talaria implements the WRITTEN intent (the roster path, 2434-2497) against `AppModel.bots`, which is a stored property and cannot miss. Demo mode and offline return the draft untouched, which is upstream's own failure rule (8263-8265): a mention must never block a send. |

Send it with exactly one terminal call, run with background=true AND notify_on_complete=true (the recipient may take minutes; the user must not be blocked):\n" then one backticked line per bot: `hermes -p '<shellQuote(name)>' chat --in ~ -c "Bot Chat" --create-if-missing -Q -q "Message from 🤖 <shellDoubleQuote(senderName)> (@<shellDoubleQuote(botHandle(live.name))>): <your composed message>"` then "\nAfter dispatching, tell the user the message was sent and END YOUR TURN — do not wait or poll; when the background process completes, its notification carries the reply — relay it then, attributed to that agent. Two words change from upstream (Desktop→Talaria, Connections→the gateway); "do not switch Gateway" is kept verbatim because switching tears down the reply watch (`detachA2ARouter`). Like upstream, the note is APPENDED and the handles are never rewritten out of the message (8319). The manual HandoffSheet stays as the deliberate mobile affordance it always was. |

'local'}. For each profile: skip nameless rows and skip isActiveRosterBot(profile, live). handle = botHandle(profile.name, profile). ### Region 5 — Agent-to-agent delegation and group rooms

**63 rows** — ⭕ 30 missing · 🔶 15 partial · ✅ 11 present · ➖ 7 n/a on mobile

Bot Mode's agent-to-agent layer is four interlocking mechanisms, not one feature. (1) **Mention-driven handoff**: an `@handle` typed in ANY composer is resolved against the live roster by a composer *middleware* that rewrites the outgoing draft, appending a bracketed instruction block telling the active agent to compose-and-dispatch a `hermes -p <bot> chat --in ~ -c "Bot Chat" --create-if-missing -Q -q "Message from 🤖 <sender> (@<handle>): …"` call with `background=true, notify_on_complete=true`, then END ITS TURN and relay the reply when the background notification lands. Every interpolation is shell-escaped (a poisoned ui_meta title is a proven RCE vector — tests/mention-handoff-quoting.test.mjs). (2) **Cross-connection delivery**: bots on OTHER registered gateways are never reached by CLI — the desktop itself resolves the remote bot's canonical Bot Chat (pinned ui_meta id → resume-by-title → create hidden), `prompt.submit`s the same attributed prefix, polls 2s/180s for a new assistant message, and relays it back as two toasts. (3) **Attribution on receipt**: `A2A_RE`/`A2A_PREFIX_RE` classify any message opening with the delivery prefix as bot-to-bot; the receiving roster row strips the prefix, paints the preview accent-italic, and pins a `🤖 @sender` chip, while a roster-poll watermark raises an unread dot (and an opt-in toast) for EVERY delivery path — RPC, CLI, cron, another machine. (4) **Group rooms**: a plugin-owned shared log where a deterministic `@mention` parse since the last user message picks who speaks each of ≤3 rotated round-robin rounds, `(pass)` is silence, per-member watermarks feed each bot only the delta, and a member writing `@user` raises a "needs you" badge. Server-side, `bot_mode_probe.py` now injects the whole protocol into the Bot Chat system prompt (`bot_mode_protocol: true` on profiles.list), superseding the plugin's SOUL.md backfill.

Talaria has the RECEIVING half in good shape and the SENDING half largely absent. The `A2AMessage` model, the `Agent Inbox` tab (three themed voices, from → to rows, broadcast handling, entrance stagger), the attribution regexes (`a2aSender`/`strippedA2A` at AppModelLive+Feeds.swift:953/967 — faithful ports of A2A_RE/A2A_PREFIX_RE), the transcript-scan feed builder, and a manual From→To→message HandoffSheet all exist and work. `botHandle`/`displayName`/`showsHandle` and the `ui_meta["hermes-bots"]` cosmetics bridge are correctly ported and verified present. What is missing is everything mention-driven: no `@` autocomplete in the composer, no mention middleware (typing `@ops fix this` sends those literal characters to the model with no handoff instruction), no roster-mention resolution, no ambiguity/`@name-device` handling, no cross-connection delivery or reply relay, no group rooms at all, no `🤖 @sender` chip or prefix-stripping on the roster preview, no roster-poll unread watermark (unread only rises from live events on sessions this app has attached, so a CLI-delivered DM to an unopened bot is invisible), and `BotModeMeta.pinnedChat` is parsed but never used — so tapping a bot opens its most-recent session rather than the canonical Bot Chat the A2A message was actually delivered into. Talaria also writes handoffs into a session titled "Agent Inbox" while every upstream sender writes "Bot Chat"; the reader tolerates both, but the writer forks the thread.

Send it with exactly one terminal call, run with background=true AND notify_on_complete=true (the recipient may take minutes; the user must not be blocked):\n" + one backticked hermes command per mentioned bot + "\nAfter dispatching, tell the user the message was sent and END YOUR TURN — do not wait or poll; when the background process completes, its notification carries the reply — relay it then, attributed to that agent. ### Region 6 — Notifications and activity

**63 rows** — ⭕ 14 missing · 🔶 17 partial · ✅ 27 present · ➖ 5 n/a on mobile

**Updated 2026-08-18 by Phase D.** Durable per-gateway watermarks, the toast bus,
the default-off activity-toast preference and bell control, mutation/context feedback,
A2A outcomes, routine/profile/session/model narration, gateway-native keyed notices,
background completion, and the forever-chat reset guard are implemented. Notices whose
triggers belong to unbuilt group-room or full-federation surfaces remain with Phases E/F.

Desktop Bot Mode's notification/activity system is a **poll-derived watermark model**, not an event-derived one: every 5s `profiles.list` returns each profile's `last_session.last_active`, and `trackInboundActivity` diffs that against a per-bot watermark. Talaria now carries the same signal in a durable per-gateway store, which is the phone-shaped version: it survives process eviction without letting same-named profiles on two gateways acknowledge each other. The unread badge is unconditional; the in-app activity toast is a persisted, default-off second layer with a roster bell control. A single themed toast queue handles keyed optimistic→confirmed mutations, gateway-native sticky/TTL notices, contextual failures and background completions, while the 200-entry local Activity journal keeps the durable account. Remaining gaps in this region are concentrated in features outside Phase D itself—group-room notices, full cross-gateway federation, and a few unrelated roster-craft rows.

| Feature | Plugin source | Behavior | Talaria | Effort | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Unread cleared on roster row open** | `plugin.js:3915-3919` | In BotRow.open, immediately after `$selectedBot.set(bot.name)` and the remoteSource early-return, and BEFORE the async prepareBotSource/openBotCanonicalChat work: `if (!bot.remoteSource && $botUnread.get()[bot.name]) { delete next[bot.name]; $botUnread.set(next) }`. Cleared optimistically — the dot disappears even if the chat open later fails. | ⭕ missing<br>`TalariaUI/Screens/RosterView.swift:257-261` | S · critical | BUG in Talaria: the roster row tap does `model.selectedTab = .home; model.openBotID = bot.id` — a raw write. `openChat(botID:)` (AppModelLive.swift:235-240) is the only path that clears `unread`/`mentionsYou`, and ChatView.onAppear (ChatView.swift:117) does not clear either. So the primary way a user opens a chat never drains its badge, and the Bots tab badge (RootView.swift:93) accumulates forever. Same raw-write bug in PushCoordinator.swift:209/256 (deep link + notification tap) and RootView.swift:498 (banner tap). |

Every later call badges any bot whose ts strictly exceeds its stored watermark. Switching gateways keeps the old gateway's watermarks: a same-named bot on the new gateway with an OLDER last_active silently won't badge; one with a NEWER timestamp badges immediately without a re-seed (i.e. it can badge on the very first poll of the new gateway). | ✅ present, deliberately divergent<br>`TalariaUI/AppModelLive+Unread.swift` | S · medium | Fixed rather than copied, in both axes. Per-GATEWAY: every mark lives under a scope key of the gateway's own URL, re-scoped on both edges of a connection, so a same-named profile on another machine can never inherit or acknowledge marks that are not its own. And DURABLE, which is the one place this file knowingly leaves upstream: `watermarksSeeded` is a per-mount flag on a laptop that reloads every few days, but a phone is killed minutes after backgrounding, so a per-launch seed would declare the entire backlog read on every cold start — exactly the population the model exists to catch. "Seeded" here means *this phone has seen this gateway's roster at least once*. |

### Region 7 — Plugin/shell integration

**59 rows** — ⭕ 16 missing · 🔶 22 partial · ✅ 10 present · ➖ 11 n/a on mobile

Bot Mode is a *plugin*, and almost everything in this region is the seam between it and the Hermes desktop shell. The whole surface is five contributions (`ctx.register` × 5: one `composer.atCompletions` source, one `composer.middleware` handler, two `panes`, one `palette` row), three `ctx.storage` keys (`bot-meta`, `activity-toasts`, `group-chats`), two `host.state` subscriptions (`profile`, `gateway`), and one main-area tile door (`host.openWorkspace`). It registers **zero** keybinds and **zero** routes/themes/statusbar/titlebar items. Talaria's shell is a fixed five-tab SwiftUI graph (`RootView.swift`) rather than a contribution registry — so the *plumbing* rows (pane docking, registry namespacing, plugin enable/disable, hover pre-warm, tooltips, injected CSS) are legitimately `na-mobile` and their iOS analogues already exist. What is genuinely missing is the *user-visible* half of the shell integration: (1) the entire `@mention` pipeline — no composer completion source, no middleware rewrite, no bot-to-bot handoff instruction, no remote delivery; (2) the `/new`→`/compact` forever-chat guard; (3) the bot-row **context menu** (7 items: Pin, Sessions, Edit Profile, Move to group, Duplicate, New chat, Delete) — Talaria's roster rows have no long-press at all, and Delete is documented as CLI-only; (4) group chats and their main-window tab lifecycle, in full; (5) the always-hidden Bot-Mode-session policy (`session.set_hidden` / `include_hidden`) which Talaria never sends, so Talaria will show every group-room and canonical chat as an ordinary session; (6) the writable half of the `ui_meta["hermes-bots"]` bridge — Talaria *reads* desktop cosmetics but writes its own `ui_meta["talaria"]` block, and ignores the `chat` canonical-chat pin entirely; (7) the in-app toast stack (`host.notify`) and haptics. Rough total: ~28 missing / ~19 partial / ~7 present / ~9 na-mobile.

Behavior: rescan the document (including shadow roots, `walkMathFaces`) at 1Hz; observe every face with an IntersectionObserver and paint only VISIBLE ones; go dormant when zero faces are mounted or zero are visible, and wake on mount (`BotFace` calls `startFaceClock()` on every render) or when one scrolls in. Newer shells use the SDK's `createBudgetedLoop(paint,{fps:15, idleWhen})` which adds hidden/minimized/unfocused pause; older shells use a hand-rolled rAF capped at 15fps that skips while `document.hidden`. `stopFaceClock` cancels the frame, disconnects the observer, and drops every cached node — wired to `ctx.onDispose` because before that the loop ran until app restart. | 🔶 partial<br>`Sources/TalariaUI/Screens/RosterView.swift:108` | S · medium | Talaria uses per-view TimelineView/animation (RosterView.swift:490 BlinkingCursor at 0.5s, :109-112 a 1s roster ticker, WorkingPulse) — SwiftUI coalesces these better than raw rAF, so a single global clock is unnecessary. What IS missing is the visibility/dormancy discipline: LazyVStack keeps off-screen rows from rendering, but the 1s ticker in RosterView runs regardless of whether the roster tab is even selected. Weaker in two ways: (a) you cannot see routines and the chat at once; (b) routines are only reachable from inside a chat, whereas desktop shows them beside every screen. iOS analogue for 'always visible': a routines summary strip in the chat header or a bot-sheet section. |

In-flight sets guard against duplicate requests; a failed push removes the guard so it retries. Writes on the Edit dialog only fire set_asset when the image actually CHANGED (235) — a no-op `clear` from one machine could race another machine's just-pushed avatar and wipe it server-side. | 🔶 partial<br>`Sources/TalariaUI/AppModelLive+Profiles.swift:21` | M · medium | Talaria has both RPCs (GatewayClient.swift:267/272) and an avatar cache (AppModelLive+Profiles.swift:21) but only pulls on demand; it never backfills the server from a locally-rendered avatar, and has no equivalent of the 160px-backfill sniff. Since Talaria draws vector avatars (AvatarView) the backfill idea applies directly: rasterize once so desktop notices can show the same face. |
