---

## 4. Design notes

The tables say *what*. This section says *how it should feel*, and it is the reason the document
exists — every craft detail the audit recovered from the plugin, grouped. Numbers here are the
plugin's actual constants, not approximations. Where Talaria already exceeds the reference, that
is called out and kept.

### 4.1 Avatar language

- **Two vocabularies, not one.** Desktop ships 7 legacy shapes + 5 platonic solids + procedural
  sigils (`shape: 'sigil-N'`) against Talaria's 6 silhouettes, and 10 `AVATAR_COLORS` against
  Talaria's 6 hues. Full engine parity is *not* recommended — Talaria's `AvatarSilhouette`
  (percentage polygons, `AvatarView.swift:8-41`) is the architecturally right answer on iOS. Port
  the **shape roster**, not the machinery (plugin.js:917-971).
- **Never drop a shape token from the parser** (plugin.js:697-786, 1310-1325). Legacy geometry is
  preserved forever upstream. `BotModeMeta.talariaShape` returns nil for unknown strings and falls
  back to a hash-derived shape — better is to keep the raw string on `Bot` so a re-save
  round-trips unchanged instead of silently rewriting a desktop user's `cloud` to `circle`.
- **The primary profile gets a fixed friendly violet squircle**, never a hash-derived near-black
  square — the same special-case as *Hermes* / *@hermes*.
- **Eyes and catchlights invert on dark bodies.** Perceptual luminance
  `0.2126R + 0.7152G + 0.0722B < 110` ⇒ dark. Dark body → cream pupils `rgba(232,220,195,0.95)`
  with a **dark** catchlight `rgba(0,0,0,0.6)`; light body → near-black pupils `rgba(0,0,0,0.85)`
  with a white catchlight `rgba(255,255,255,0.85)`. The catchlight follows the *pupil's* contrast,
  not the body's — a white sparkle on a cream pupil is invisible and reads as "no eyes" on
  maroon/ink/oxblood avatars.
- **Catchlights ride the pupils** at offset `(-0.6, -0.7)`, `r=0.65`, repositioned every frame
  (plugin.js:1061-1075). Pinning them to a fixed eye line makes them drift outside the cloud
  shape's lower-set eyes. The eye baseline is shape-aware from the very first paint (cloud 22,
  everything else 17.2) so eyes never visibly jump on the first clock tick.
- **Closed eyes are not scaled pupils**: blinking hides the open group (`opacity 0`) and shows a
  separate 2px-stroke round-capped line spanning ±2.6 either side of each eye center, in the pupil
  color. `scaleY` works on Talaria's rounded-rect eye, but a lash line reads warmer at small sizes.
- **Portrait vs face**: a stored image renders as a plain `<img>` at 22% border-radius,
  `objectFit: cover` — the animated face is only for vector shape/color bots.
- **Provenance is encoded in pixel dimensions**, not a flag (plugin.js:374-392): 160×160 = a
  machine-rasterized copy of the vector face (for inter-agent notices only), 96×104 = a pet frame,
  256 = a user upload. It reads as a hack and it survives round-trips through an asset store that
  carries no metadata. The roster explicitly *refuses* to display a fetched 160×160 PNG (it sniffs
  the PNG IHDR) — parking that raster on the row would kill the live face. **Any client that both
  uploads and downloads profile avatars needs this guard or bots slowly freeze into stills.**
- **Group avatars are composites**: up to three member faces at size 20 fanned with `-10px`
  overlap and a 2px ring in the panel background color (the Discord group-DM icon), up to six at
  `-6px` in the room header, falling back to a bare `organization` glyph when the room is
  unseated. Tooltip is the comma-joined display names.
- **Selection chrome is "ring, not fill"** — shapes `ring-1` accent, colors `ring-2` accent with a
  1px offset, pets `ring-1`. Talaria's per-theme version is better (ink's parchment-gap + ink-halo
  swatch ring, `CreateBotView.swift:416-425`, is the nicest single detail in either codebase) —
  but the *shape* row should get the same treatment as the hue row; it currently just tints the fill.

### 4.2 Motion — two moods, and only two

- **`idle` and `work`.** Idle is a barely-there sway: turn `sin(t*0.5)*1.5°`, tilt `sin(t*0.27)°`,
  roll `sin(t*0.85)*1.2°`, plus an SVG-level rotate about transform-origin `50% 70%`
  (plugin.js:999-1025, 1091). It reads as breathing, not bouncing. Work is a persistent **-11°
  forward lean** with an 8° sway. *A bot leaning into its work is the single most legible status
  cue in the roster — much more than a spinner.*
- **Blink cadence carries state.** Idle blink is `t % 3.2 > 3.02` — a hard 180 ms shut every 3.2 s,
  in lockstep across all faces on one shared clock. Working blink is `t % 1.45 > 1.26` — 190 ms
  every 1.45 s: **a thinking bot blinks twice as often.** Talaria blinks on a per-avatar random
  3.6–5.2 s timer with eased 90 ms close / 120 ms open (`AvatarView.swift:108`) — desynchronized,
  which is arguably nicer, but it loses the tell. Halve the interval when working.
- **Working tells stack, four at once**: eyes grow (`ry` 2.6 vs 2.3, plugin.js:1362); gaze drifts
  (`gazeX = sin(t*0.55)*3.6`, `gazeY = -1.6 + sin(t*0.38)*2` — it looks around, slightly downward,
  like reading); the head leans (`turn = -11 + sin(t*0.48)*8`); and **three dots pulse in sequence
  below the chin**, 0.7 rad phase offset each, drawn at `cy 41.2` in a viewBox deliberately taller
  than wide (40×44) to hold them. The dots are the one desktop element with no Talaria equivalent
  and the clearest at-a-glance "thinking" cue.
- **The work pose is conditional, never ambient** (plugin.js:3852-3856): it applies to the active
  profile *while the gateway is busy*, **or** any bot whose `last_session.last_active` falls inside
  the liveness window. Explicitly not "every bot whenever the gateway is busy". Talaria's
  `WorkingPulse` should follow the same two-clause rule.
- **Two animation constants to copy exactly**: the idle bob is `translateY(0 → -3px)`
  (plugin.js:8053); the working pulse is `opacity 1 → 0.35 → 1 over 1.2s ease-in-out infinite` on a
  1.5px dot (plugin.js:601-602). Talaria's `glowPulse` runs at period 2.3 s — roughly half speed.
  Pick one on purpose. Slow enough to read as breathing, not blinking.
- **Liveness has a number**: `ACTIVE_WINDOW_S = 90` (plugin.js:3823-3825). It drives both the
  pulsing dot (`title: 'Active in the last 90s'`) and the avatar's work pose.
- **Entrance is Talaria's invention and it is better.** Desktop has no row entrance animation —
  rows just appear on the 5 s poll repaint; the whole motion budget goes into the faces. Talaria's
  staggered fade+rise (`RosterView.swift:501`, 0.045 s × index, easeOut 0.45, y +12 → 0) should be
  kept — but **cap the stagger** (`min(index, 12) * 0.045`, as `AgentInboxView` already does at
  0.055) or a 40-bot roster spends 1.8 s finishing, and **make sure it never replays on a roster
  refresh** — keep the `ForEach` id as `bot.id`, never index. Motion that reads as arrival for the
  first screenful must never read as a slow load for row 60. Inherit the same curve for any new
  surface (Active-now strip, toast queue).
- **Dormancy is a feature.** One shared clock for all faces, 15 fps cap ("smooth at avatar scale"),
  an `IntersectionObserver` so off-screen faces cost nothing, the clock parking itself when zero
  faces are mounted *or* zero are visible, a 1 Hz rescan to pick up new faces, full teardown on
  dispose (plugin.js:1109-1289). The stated cost model: *a disabled plugin or an empty roster costs
  zero frames.* Talaria's per-avatar `Task { while !Task.isCancelled … }` blink loop is spawned in
  `onAppear` and never cancelled, and its 1 s roster ticker (`RosterView.swift:108-113`) runs
  regardless of selected tab or scene phase. Fine at 10 bots; not at 100.

### 4.3 Ordering and ranking

- **The sort rule, exactly** (plugin.js:7637-7666): pinned bots float to the top **as a group**;
  inside each group, recency wins, where recency = `max(bot-meta created ms, last_session.last_active × 1000)`.
  Group-chat rows interleave with unpinned bots in the same single ranking — flat, Discord-shaped,
  no section headers.
- **A freshly created bot tops the roster** until someone else gets a message. That is deliberate:
  a new teammate is where you left it.
- **There is no reserved slot for the primary bot.** Hermes competes on recency like everyone else.
- **Search narrows, never re-ranks** (plugin.js:2960-2981) — asserted in `tests/bots-search.test.mjs`,
  which also pins that a blank query returns the *same array reference*. Results that reshuffle
  while you type feel broken; the user's mental map of the list must survive typing.
- **Search is forgiving; autocomplete is strict.** `filterBots` does case-insensitive **substring**
  matching across four fields — display name, raw profile name, `@handle`, and connection label —
  and strips a leading `@` so typing `@hermes` works. The completion provider does case-insensitive
  **prefix** matching on the *handle only*, capped at 8. The asymmetry is intentional: search is
  exploration; completion inserts a token that must be valid. The search field only renders when
  the roster is non-empty.
- **Ranking one-liners elsewhere follow the same shape** — "likeliest first, ties keep prior
  order": pets rank installed → curated → rest.
- **Presence never reorders.** `activeBots` preserves input order, the Active-now strip is purely
  additive, and the list below never re-sorts because a bot woke up (plugin.js:3829-3830,
  6883-6937). On a touch surface this is a correctness rule, not a nicety: a list that reshuffles
  under a thumb mid-tap is the classic mobile failure.

### 4.4 Badges, presence and attribution

- **Two layers, and only the first is mandatory.** The unread dot *always* fires; the toast is a
  second, opt-in layer, default **off**, with the reasoning written into the source — cron runs and
  bot-to-bot chatter turn a busy roster into a firehose, and the badge already carries the signal
  (plugin.js:133 vs 137). Port both the layering and the default. On a phone the case is stronger,
  and Talaria's persistent ledger already gives a durable record.
- **Two dots, two meanings, fixed order** (plugin.js:3999-4016): unread renders first — 8 px,
  solid accent, no animation, `aria-label='unread'` — then active-now — 6 px accent with the
  1.2 s pulse and tooltip *"Active in the last 90s"* — then the relative timestamp. Both can show
  at once. **Solid and bigger = you have something to read; smaller and breathing = it is alive
  right now.** Never merge them into one indicator.
- **Badge hierarchy: blocked outranks unread.** Talaria's tab bar already encodes this (alert tone
  for approvals vs unread tone for traffic, `TalariaTabBar.swift:177-181`) and the row badge lets
  an approval's `!` replace the count (`RosterView.swift:402-404`). Keep it — a bot waiting on you
  is categorically louder than a bot that talked.
- **Watermarks are seeded, not zeroed.** The first roster poll records every bot's `last_active`
  and badges nothing; only later movement badges. Otherwise a cold launch marks ancient history
  unread. The watermark is written monotonically (`max(prev, ts)`) *before* the seeding guard, so a
  clock going backwards can never lower it.
- **Never badge, and never toast, the bot that is on screen** (plugin.js:129). Talaria extends the
  same courtesy to the ledger for plain turn-completions while still journaling mentions
  (`AppModelLive+Feeds.swift:389`). That asymmetry is right: *"it finished"* is redundant when you
  watched it; *"someone addressed you"* is not.
- **Clear optimistically, on the first frame.** Unread is deleted the instant the row is tapped,
  before any async open work (plugin.js:3915 precedes the awaits at 3924/3932). The dot must never
  linger through a slow session resume.
- **One clear-path, enforced.** Desktop duplicates the clear in two open handlers and drifts (the
  group and sessions routes never clear). iOS should collapse every route — row tap, push tap,
  deep link, banner, palette, activity row — into a single `openChat(botID:)` and treat a raw
  `openBotID` write as a bug. That exact class of drift is live today at `RosterView.swift:260`,
  `PushCoordinator.swift:209/256` and `RootView.swift:498`.
- **The bot-to-bot preview is the signature roster affordance.** When the preview opens with
  `Message from 🤖 <name> (@handle):` (or the legacy `Message from agent '<name>':`) the row
  changes register: the prefix is **stripped**, the remaining text renders *italic in the accent
  color* (not muted tertiary), and a pill is appended — rounded-full, chrome-hover background,
  accent text, 0.625 rem semibold, contents `['🤖', '@handle']`, tooltip *"Last message came from
  @X (bot-to-bot)"*. If stripping leaves nothing, the preview is the single character `…`. **This
  is what makes the roster read as a team rather than a list of tools**, and porting the chip is
  the highest-craft, lowest-effort win in the whole document. Per pack: soft = pill, control =
  `[@OPS]` bracket mono, ink = small-caps *from ops*.
- **Machinery is never shown to the user.** The delivery prefix is a protocol string; every place
  it could surface — roster preview, generated session title, inbox row body — strips it first.
- **Previews are markdown-flattened, never raw** (plugin.js:2994): fenced code → space, `` `inline` ``
  → bare text, images/links → their label text, `**bold**`/`__bold__`/`*em*`/`_em_`/`~~strike~~`
  unwrapped, leading `#` headings and `>` quotes dropped, whitespace collapsed, trimmed. Without
  this, rows show literal asterisks and backticks. The plugin fixed this specifically so rows would
  read "like Discord's".
- **Preview identity must equal click identity** (plugin.js:3863-3867, hermes-agent#88200). The row
  previews `bot.preferred_session ?? bot.last_session`. Liveness and the timestamp still read
  `last_session` — *any* recent activity means the bot is alive. Two different questions, two
  different sources. Getting this wrong means the row quotes a cron run while the tap opens a
  different conversation, which is the single most disorienting bug a roster can have.
- **The pinned marker** is a literal 📌 at 0.6875 rem in quaternary text, tooltip *"Pinned"*,
  sitting **before** the display name inside the truncating name cluster and marked `shrink-0` so it
  survives truncation. Pinning is a plain boolean that rides `ui_meta` to every machine — pinning on
  the laptop pins on the phone.
- **The group "needs you" badge** is a separate, stronger signal than unread: an accent pill,
  white text, 0.6 rem semibold, title *"A bot in this room needs your input"*, fired when a member's
  message matches `/@user\b/i` (plugin.js:3367-3370, 7586-7592).
- **Active-now is a separate, announced strip**: a horizontal `ACTIVE NOW` row of 24 px faces +
  names *above* the list, `role='status' aria-live='polite'`, whose chips open the canonical chat
  through the exact same code path as a row tap. It is presence, not a filter — the same bots still
  appear below.
- **Zero-height when idle.** The strip returns null rather than rendering a "nothing active"
  placeholder (plugin.js:6891). Empty states earn their space only when the surface is the whole
  screen; inline status bars should vanish. If ported, animate the appearance/disappearance (spring
  insert) and fire an accessibility announcement on membership change.
- **Dedupe by subject, not by time.** Talaria's ledger keys rows (`approval:<id>`,
  `cron-run:<job>:<ts>`, `a2a:<bot>:<hash>`) so a later state update rewrites the same row rather
  than appending a second (`AppModelLive+Feeds.swift:180-186`). The gateway's own
  `notification.show` / `notification.clear` uses the same key-supersede idea (ws-protocol.md:260-261) —
  a toast queue should honor it too.

### 4.5 Empty, error and stale states

- **Four states, in this priority** (plugin.js:7651-7706, 7856-7878):
  1. loading with no cached roster → centered `GlyphSpinner` "breathe";
  2. error with no cached roster → a small grid card whose copy branches on socket state —
     gateway **up**: *"Roster unavailable: `<message>`. If your gateway predates profiles.list,
     update Hermes and restart the gateway."*; gateway **down**: *"Waiting for the gateway
     connection… (remote gateways can take a few seconds; retries automatically)"* — plus a
     secondary **Retry now** button;
  3. roster genuinely empty → `EmptyState` icon `hubot`, title **"No agents yet"**, description
     **"Create your first teammate."**;
  4. error *but a cached roster exists* → render the **old roster** plus an inline notice bar
     *"Roster refresh failed — showing the last good list."* (+ *" Waiting for the gateway to
     reconnect…"* when the socket is down).
- **State 4 is the resilience rule: a failed refresh must never blank a roster the user already
  had.** The full error card is reserved for "never had a roster at all". This matters more on
  cellular than on desktop.
- **No-match is a separate, announced state**, not the empty state: `role='status'`,
  `aria-live='polite'`, centered, curly quotes — *No bots match “`<trimmed query>`”*. The group
  picker varies it by context: *"No bots yet — create agents first."* when the query is blank.
- **Every empty state is a next action, never a statement of absence** (verbatim): roster →
  *"No agents yet" / "Create your first teammate."*; group room → *"No messages yet — say hi to the
  room"*; bot with no history → *"No conversations yet — say hi"*; session with no messages →
  *"No messages yet"*; a one-member room's peer list → *"no one else yet"*; an empty SOUL teammate
  roster → *"- (none yet)"*; an A2A message that strips to nothing → `…`.
- **Empty states name the mechanism and the escape hatch**: *"No pets in the petdex gallery. Run
  `hermes pets` to explore."*, *"No image model available. If you just enabled one (or updated
  Hermes), restart the gateway…"*, *"Capability catalog needs a newer gateway (restart it after
  updating Hermes)."* Talaria's `CopyPack` does this well in three voices — keep drilling on it, and
  **drop** desktop-only remedies (`Ctrl+K → "Restart gateway"`) rather than translating them.
- **Session-browser empties always say which kind of empty**: *"No stored sessions yet."* vs *"No
  sessions match that filter."* vs *"No matching sessions in the 200 most recent."*
- **Shown-but-disabled beats hidden**: "New Group Chat" stays visible and disabled until there are
  ≥2 local bots (plugin.js:7765), so the capability is discoverable before it is usable.

### 4.6 Copy, toasts and confirmations

- **Silence is a design decision, and it is three-way.** `saveBotMeta` distinguishes
  `persisted` / `unsupported` / `failed` precisely so an old gateway does not toast an error on
  every single save forever (plugin.js:246-254). "Legacy backend" and "something broke" are
  different facts and only one deserves attention. Generally: **failure is silent when it is
  expected, loud when it is not.**
- **Name what *did* save.** A cosmetic save that persists locally but fails remotely says exactly
  that — *"Saved look locally; remote persistence failed"* (plugin.js:5004) — never a bare
  "save failed".
- **Announce the slow thing, then announce its result.** Duplicate fires *"Duplicating `<name>`…"*
  immediately and *"Created `<name>` — full copy of `<base>`"* on success, `notifyError('Duplicate failed')`
  otherwise (plugin.js:4077-4086). The optimistic first toast is what makes a 3-second clone feel
  instant. Destructive buttons use the same rhythm: **Delete → Deleting… → Deleted**. Other pairs:
  *"Draft agent \"`<slug>`\" discarded"*, *"Deleted profile `<name>`"*, *"`<Name>` pinned to top"* /
  *"`<Name>` unpinned"*.
- **Two-stage feedback on every dispatch.** A remote DM toasts the moment it leaves —
  *"Messaged @ops on Mac Mini — will relay the reply here"* — a second when the reply lands (title
  *"🤖 Ops (Mac Mini)"*, body truncated to 500 chars), and a distinct third if nothing came back —
  *"No reply from @ops yet — check its Bot Chat on Mac Mini"* (info, not error). **Delegation only
  feels trustworthy when the user is told it left *and* told when it returns.** Talaria does the
  first only, and only as a feed row.
- **Destructive confirms name the collateral.** *"Delete bot and profile?"* → *"This will
  permanently delete the bot `<name>` and its associated Hermes profile at `<path>`. This cannot be
  undone."* — name in medium foreground, **path in mono**, which is what makes "cannot be undone"
  land instead of reading as boilerplate (`profiles.list` already returns `path`). Disband is
  softer and says what **survives**: the grouping is removed from N bots and the room log cleared,
  but the bots and their `Group: <name>` sessions are kept and stay reachable.
- **The forever-chat promise has its own voice.** *"This chat never resets — Bot chats are one
  continuous conversation — compacting instead. For a throwaway session with this agent, use
  Sessions mode."* (plugin.js:8232-8238). It explains the substitution **and** names the escape
  hatch in the same breath. Do not shorten it into an error. It is the best notification in the
  file, because it saves the user's forever-chat and teaches the mental model in one line —
  **notifications can prevent mistakes, not just report them.**
- **Refusal is an explanation, not a failure.** Tapping a bot on another machine does not switch
  gateways; it toasts, titled with that bot's display name: *"Stay in this chat and @`<handle>` to
  message them. Gateway stays on this device."* When a row can't be opened, say what the user *can*
  do with it instead of disabling it silently.
- **Error copy names the object, the failure, and the retry**, in second person: *"Could not open
  `<name>`'s chat — try again"*, *"Could not reach `<connectionLabel>`"*, *"Still on `<live>`, not
  `<target>`"*, *"Update Hermes Desktop to chat with agents on other connections."*, *"Cronjobs
  exist in this profile but none are tagged for this bot. Name a job \"[bot:`<name>`] …\" to show it
  here."* Never a bare error code; never blame. **Adopt this as house style across all three packs.**
- **Notifications are titled by identity, not by event.** Every `host.notify` in the region uses
  `displayName(bot)` as the title and puts the verb in the body — so the notification shade reads
  like a list of people, matching the roster. Inbound bot-to-bot: *"🤖 New message for `<Name>`"*;
  anything else: *"`<Name>` has new activity"*; body = preview truncated to 140 chars, or
  *"Open the chat to see it."*
- **Give the mute a visible home.** The activity-toast toggle is a bell / bell-slash button in the
  pane header, with copy that states the state **and** the action: *"Activity toasts on — click to
  silence"* / *"Activity toasts off — click to enable"*. A pref buried in settings gets found once;
  a bell in the header gets understood immediately.
- **Placeholders carry information rather than repeating the label.** The Title field's placeholder
  *is* the auto-derived display name (plugin.js:5079), so leaving it blank visibly means "keep the
  derived name". Name placeholder `inbox-triage`, title placeholder `Inbox Triage` — teaching the
  slug↔title relationship by example. The composer teaches its own grammar the same way: a group
  room's placeholder is *"Message `<group>`… (@name to direct, @everyone for all)"* — no help text,
  no tooltip, no onboarding.
- **Explain the consequence, not the mechanism.** The "Share keys & accounts with the main profile"
  checkbox defaults **on** with a full sub-line about token refreshes invalidating each other
  (plugin.js:5722-5729) — the one Advanced control whose default actually matters.
- **Silence in a room is framed as good.** `(pass)` is described to the model as desirable ("it lets
  the conversation settle"), a round where everyone passes **ends** the drive, and a timed-out or
  thrown turn is a pass rather than an error. Nothing in the UI scolds a quiet bot; the room simply
  stops.
- **Optimism, then reconciliation.** Routine toggles paint a pending overlay and drop it when the
  server catches up; a sent handoff inserts its row immediately and clears the sweep throttle so the
  stored version (with the reply) replaces it. Talaria already does the handoff half well — mirror
  the pattern for any future mention send.

### 4.7 Identity and disambiguation

- **Two identities, two weights.** Bold display name; quiet mono `@handle` at 0.6875 rem in
  quaternary, baseline-aligned, **only when they differ**. The handle is never the loud thing.
  Talaria matches this (`RosterView.swift:319`) but inverts it in soft/control for untitled bots,
  where the `@handle` becomes the primary label — worth fixing.
- **`default` is never shown as "default".** Three helpers enforce it — `displayName` → *Hermes*,
  `botHandle` → *hermes*, `groupSpeakerLabel` → *Hermes*. On iOS this must be **one** source of
  truth so a fourth surface can't leak the raw id; `BotSheetView.swift:57` currently rolls its own
  rule and ignores `title`.
- **Ambiguity refuses rather than guesses.** In prose, a bare name shared by two bots resolves to
  **nothing** — the user must type `@dixie-mac-mini`. Silently picking one would send a real message
  to the wrong machine. The poison is sticky: once a form is ambiguous, a third bot cannot claim it.
  Inside a group room the *opposite* rule applies — last-writer-wins, first names, punctuation
  insensitive — because the member set is small and known.
- **Code is not prose.** Mention parsing strips ```` ```fences``` ```` and `` `inline code` ``
  before matching, and requires the `@` to sit at string start or after whitespace — so `user@host`,
  an email, or a shell variable never fires a handoff. **Any iOS port must keep both guards; a
  false-positive handoff is a message actually sent.**
- **Device labels do double duty** — one string, five appearances: the chip on the row (tooltip
  *"Lives on Homelab"*), the fourth search field (typing `homelab` finds every bot there), the
  suffix in autocomplete meta lines, the bracket in group transcripts (`[Spark]`), and the name of a
  peer machine's untitled `default` agent. Consistent enough that a user learns it without being told.
- **Click to disambiguate, not always-on.** Room speakers show a plain display name; tapping one
  expands to `<Name>-<Source> (@handle)` (plugin.js:7228-7331). The clutter of full identity is paid
  only when asked for — the same instinct as `showsHandle`. On touch this is strictly better than
  desktop's hover tooltips, and it is worth adopting for Talaria's Agent Inbox rows too.
- **Foreign rows are read-only and say so** — no context menu at all, not a disabled one; local
  metadata is never painted onto them (two "default" agents must never borrow each other's title,
  color or pin); a remote row whose profile name is literally `default` displays as its **connection
  label** so two machines' primaries are tellable apart.

### 4.8 Touch craft, performance and accessibility

- **Haptics mark departure only.** `haptic('tap')` fires as the *first statement* of exactly three
  handlers — open a bot's chat, open a group room, open a bot from the Active-now strip
  (plugin.js:3900, 7532, 7783) — before state, before I/O. Not on toggles, not on menu items.
  Talaria has none at all; adopt the same restraint. Three theme packs are three haptic voices
  waiting to happen: soft = light impact, control = selection click, ink = a heavier stamp.
- **Pre-warm on approach.** `onPointerEnter` warms the profile's backend before the click lands
  (plugin.js:3876-3897), because **the resume round-trip *is* the perceived cost of opening a bot**.
  Touch has no hover; the iOS analogues are warming on scroll-into-view for the top N rows, on
  touch-down before the navigation commits, on long-press preview, or on foreground for the
  last-opened bot.
- **Nothing about tapping a row waits on the network to feel like it happened.** Unread clears
  before the RPC; the sheet opens before the fetch.
- **Double-tap cannot fork.** A per-bot in-flight promise map (plugin.js:2740-2742) means an
  impatient user mashing a row gets one chat, not two. **Coalesce on the bot id, not on the view.**
- **Stale taps die at a gateway swap.** A generation counter is captured before the await and
  re-checked after it (plugin.js:6766-6776), so a resume that resolves against the wrong backend
  does nothing rather than binding the wrong session.
- **Staged, never written on pick.** Everything in the avatar dialog is dialog-local until Save —
  pet selection is explicitly staged with the comment that a direct meta write "gets clobbered by
  Save's own image state". Talaria's portrait generate and clear write straight to the gateway
  (`AppModelLive+Profiles.swift:340, 367`), so **Cancel does not undo them — the sheet lies about
  being cancellable.**
- **Reconcile, don't migrate.** The `session.set_hidden` sweep runs on every load *and* every
  reconnect, is idempotent, and swallows every rejection. Old rows born under a defunct pref quietly
  heal; nothing ever asks the user about it.
- **Invalidate after every mutation.** Duplicate, delete and cosmetic saves all invalidate the
  shared roster query. Talaria's `AppModel` state *is* that cache — call `refreshRoster()` after
  every roster-changing write (it already does for profile writes,
  `AppModelLive+Profiles.swift:219/287/310`).
- **Accessibility is not optional decoration**: the Active-now strip is `role='status'
  aria-live='polite'`, the search-miss is a live region, chips are real buttons titled *"Open
  `<label>`'s chat"*, and the unread dot carries `aria-label='unread'`. iOS equivalents:
  `accessibilityLabel` on the badge (*"`<n>` unread"*), an `AccessibilityNotification.Announcement`
  when the active set changes, a label on every icon-only control (`RosterView.swift:118-125`
  currently has neither label nor menu), and **never encode state in color alone**.
- **The ink pack needs its own vocabulary for state.** The pulsing `WaxSealDot`
  (`ActivityView.swift:305-322`) instead of a chip is the model: a "needs you" badge, an unread dot
  and an active-now pulse should each get an ink-native form rather than a recolored capsule. That is
  the pack's whole point.
- **When you exceed the reference, say so and keep it.** The persistent day-grouped Activity ledger,
  the tab-badge rollup, the Live Activity, real APNs push, the dirty-diff profile editor, cross-session
  FTS, session rename/delete — desktop cannot do these. **Parity means matching desktop's *signal
  fidelity*** — the watermark, the toast bus, the clear-on-open discipline, the ordering rule — **not
  throwing away the surfaces that are better on a phone.**

---

## 5. Build order

Weighted for a phone-first power user: someone who drives several agents, is away from the desktop,
and needs the phone to be *the* client rather than a viewer. Each item names its **first concrete
step** and its prerequisites.

### Phase 1 — The spine

*The model itself. Nothing above it is worth building while the phone silently forks people's
forever-chats. Items 1.1–1.3 are one contiguous piece of work; do them together.*

| # | Item | First concrete step | Prereq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1.1 | **`preferred_session_ids` round-trip** — the enabling change for the whole region | Add an optional `preferredSessionIDs: [String: String]` param to `GatewayClient.listProfiles` (`TalariaKit/GatewayClient.swift:228-231`) and parse `preferred_session {id, resolved_id, title, preview, started_at, last_active, message_count}` off each row | — |
| 1.2 | **Canonical-chat resolver** — the four branches of `openBotCanonicalChat` (grandfather / verified pin / lookup-failure / definitive-null), replacing "resume `last_session`" | Rewrite `ensureSession` (`AppModelLive.swift:250-288`) to read `BotModeMeta.pinnedChat` first; delete the `lastSessionByBot` fallback path (`AppModelLive.swift:167-169`). Add a per-bot in-flight map so a double tap cannot fork | 1.1, 1.7 (to *write* the pin) |
| 1.3 | **Birth call + `include_hidden`** — `session.create {profile, title:'Bot Chat', hidden:true}`, pin immediately, mount, 400 ms, kickoff `"Hey, tell me about yourself!"` | Add `title` and `hidden` to `GatewayClient.createSession` (`:286-293`) and `include_hidden: true` to `listSessions` (`:279-284`) — the latter is a one-line change that makes `SessionsSheet` structurally able to see Bot Mode's own sessions at all | 1.2 |
| 1.4 | **One `openChat()` door** — a live correctness bug, not just parity | Change `RosterView.swift:257-261` from the raw `model.openBotID = bot.id` write to `model.openChat(botID:)`; then `PushCoordinator.swift:209/256` and `RootView.swift:498`. Clear unread *before* the async open | — |
| 1.5 | **Roster watermark unread** — the only model that catches cron, CLI, another machine, or anything that happened while the phone slept | Add `[String: Double]` watermarks + a `seeded` flag to `LiveRuntime`, diff `last_session.last_active` inside `refreshRoster` (`AppModelLive.swift:166`), skip the open bot, write monotonically | 1.4 |
| 1.6 | **Recency ordering + pinned block** — "the single biggest felt difference from desktop" | Sort the roster by `(pinned desc, max(created, last_active) desc)` at the `AppModelLive.swift:166` assembly point rather than painting `profiles.list` order | 1.7 for `pinned`/`created` |
| 1.7 | **Symmetric `ui_meta["hermes-bots"]` write** with the three-way `persisted`/`unsupported`/`failed` outcome | Change `CreateBotView.swift:728-730` (and `AppModelLive+Profiles.swift:237`) to write `hermes-bots` — title, shape, color mapped back to the nearest `AVATAR_COLORS` hex, `pinned`, `created`, `chat` — keeping `talaria` only for phone-only keys; inspect `result.applied.ui_meta` for the outcome | — |

---

## 6. Mobile ceiling

52 rows are marked ➖. They are not one thing — they sit on **two floors**, and only one of them is
a ceiling.

### Floor 1 — genuinely impossible or already solved better by iOS (31 rows)

**Web/DOM/rendering machinery (6).** *Math-sampled shape outlines* exist only because an SVG
outline must be re-deformed every frame; SwiftUI's answer is a static `Shape` plus a transform, and
Talaria's `AvatarSilhouette` is already the right architecture — port the shape *roster*, not the
machinery. *Scroll-container quirk fixes* are DOM workarounds; the transferable lesson is the
truncation failure mode, which `RosterView.swift:271, 363-364` already guards. *Runtime CSS
injection* has no analogue — but its two animation constants (3 pt bob, 1.2 s pulse) must be ported
exactly. *The shared face clock* is partly free from `LazyVStack` culling, with one real lesson
(cancel the per-avatar blink task, pause on scene phase). *Reset-defaults-on-dialog-close* is free
because SwiftUI rebuilds sheets per presentation. *mergeServerMeta's local-image survival* is
correct by construction, since `ProfileAssetStore`'s cache is a separate singleton.

**Hover and pointer affordances (4).** Three pre-warm rows and the tooltip layer. Touch has no
hover, but **both jobs still need doing**: pre-warm becomes warm-on-scroll-into-view / touch-down /
long-press-preview (the resume round-trip *is* the cost of opening a bot), and tooltips become
`accessibilityLabel` on every icon-only control plus long-press context menus for the affordances
tooltips were teaching.

**Plugin-host machinery (8).** The plugin identity record, `register(ctx)`, the `onDispose`
teardown contract, contribution-id namespacing, pane docking, the shared `QueryClient`, the
"unused host capabilities" negative space, and the finding that Bot Mode registers **zero
keybinds** — so no hardware-keyboard work is owed for parity. iOS analogues already exist:
`RootView.wireUp()` + `attachEventRouters()` do the register/dispose job and are idempotent per
client identity (which is *better* than upstream — plugin.js:8157-8161 leaks a listener). Three
ideas survive: same-id-replaces-in-place for keyed notifications, invalidate-after-mutation, and
event-driven roster updates instead of polling (Talaria's `sessions.changed` path is already better
for battery).

**Desktop-only shell, CLI and lifecycle hardening (10).** Shell-quoting of handoff interpolations
(twice) — Talaria delivers over `prompt.submit` and has no shell to escape into, but **the
equivalent discipline applies on the prompt side**: sanitize the sender so a hostile title cannot
forge a second attribution line (currently raw at `AppModelLive+Feeds.swift:911`). The `SOUL.md`
messaging-protocol section and its one-shot backfill are superseded server-side by
`bot_mode_probe.py`, which injects the protocol into the Bot Chat system prompt and reports
`bot_mode_protocol: true` — **Talaria should rely on that flag and never write SOUL.md from a
phone.** The `hideOwnedBotSessions` sweep matters less because the roster *is* Talaria's list — but
Talaria's sessions should still ride the hidden flag so they don't clutter a shared desktop's
sidebar. The rasterized-face guard, `pushLocalAvatars` and the legacy-shape roster become live
requirements the moment avatar upload lands (2.8/3.5). Draft-discard is the invariant that makes
lazy profile creation safe — port it *with* drafts if drafts ever land.

**Surfaces Talaria hasn't built yet (3).** *Unread cleared on an Active-now chip open*, *the strip
collapsing to zero height*, and *the group-row preview + recency key* are n/a only because the
Active-now strip and group rooms don't exist. They become requirements at 2.6 and 3.1 — the
general rule they encode (**every route into a chat clears unread**) is Phase 1 item 1.4.

### Floor 2 — n/a only because Talaria is single-gateway (21 rows)

These are **unbuilt, not impossible.** Talaria's `ConnectionRegistry` switches between saved
gateways (`switchGateway` flushes the world, `AppModelLive+Reconnect.swift:487`), so there is no
union roster and therefore no foreign rows to badge, menu, guard or route:

- `mergeMultiSourceRoster` in four parts (active-source detection, row cloning, annotate-vs-append,
  connect-on-demand row retention) — the annotate path is what makes `@name-device` appear on the
  user's *own* bots when a name collides, which is the disambiguation payoff.
- Identity primitives: `botRosterKey` = `connectionId::name`, and `botRosterMeta` never painting
  local metadata onto a foreign row (twice) — **key bot meta by `(gatewayID, profileName)`, not
  `profileName`**.
- Foreign-row behavior: no context menu, tap explains instead of navigating (four rows), never
  badging unread (two rows), and `prepareBotSource`'s activation guard (two rows) — whose
  transferable rule is *after switching connections, re-read the pin from the new gateway before
  opening anything*.
- Remote delivery: ambiguous-handle refusal, remote-mention routing, `deliverRemoteRosterMentions`,
  and "a remote row is a message target, not a chat target".

They land as a group at 3.2/3.3. Until then the correct behavior is the one Talaria already has:
one live gateway, and no pretence otherwise. What is **not** acceptable in the meantime is losing
their design rules — never carry a pin across sources, never let a row you don't own offer mutating
affordances, and never silently switch backends under a tap.
